# Dazed and Confused

Journal: `ep1/quests/minor_quest/mq303_addict` · `MinorQuest` · hash `1219120384`.

Journal premise: The dust has settled, the circus has closed down and the chimps have escaped. More specifically – Tool's gone into hiding someplace. What the hell were you thinking, telling him he's an FIA clone? Getting tangled up in this mess wasn't the best idea now, was it? Either way, the scroll's out there (a real masterpiece, mind you) and Shank paid what he was due. Another contract closed. Pleased with yourself?

**Coverage: journal-linked.** 299 exact journal-linked nodes in 11 resources; 13 resources in the inspected dependency context.

Resource roles in that context: 13 content candidate. Role labels describe path/name evidence, not proof of runtime reachability.

Root quest resources with additional journal references: none indexed. Roots remain activation context when a phase/scene anchor exists, to avoid importing sibling launch scaffolding.

Generated structural analysis from decoded resources. Shared resources are context, not exclusive quest ownership. The tables trace stored sockets and fact dependencies; they do not simulate runtime ordering, prove all branches reachable, or infer chronology from journal objective order. ## Required acceptance cases before reuse

Exercise normal completion and each named alternate outcome; interrupt and return at every scene handoff; start with spatial/item/actor predicates already satisfied; save/load across waits and joins; cancel parallel listeners; verify reward delivery once; and verify cleanup without changing the committed outcome. Apply the block-specific cases in the [expanded contracts](../all-quest-blocks.md). No installed playtest is claimed.

## Connected paths and supporting bindings

1511 repeated typed/socket path signatures in this selected graph context; 208 distinct direct resource identities; 23 embedded graph handoffs. The full occurrence lists, concrete parameters, exact source pointers and external edges are retained in the checked evidence. Shared forward dependencies can serve several quests; these are context counts.
